# Competitive board: Zeebra against external Gröbner engines

Generated by the competitive harness. Do not edit: regenerate it.

Every row is one library's attempt at one task on one problem, **including the attempts that produced no answer**. A row whose outcome is `unsupported`, `stopped-by-limit` or `failed` carries no time, and the cell where a time would be names the missing capability or the limit instead. The protocol is `docs/research/external-comparison-protocol.md`.

## Speedups over Zeebra

Computed only for rows agreeing on problem digest, task, limits, machine and build, with **both** outcomes answered. A ratio whose two answers share no checked invariant is marked: the protocol does not permit publishing it.

The **provenance** column is the corpus's own record of how the problem's ideal equivalence was established, which is the gate on quoting a figure for it at all. A problem with no recorded equivalence, or one no external engine can represent, is **refused here rather than omitted**; a ratio resting on a single external engine is published with that fact stated, because nothing can triangulate it.

**1393 pairs**: 0 refused for want of a recorded ideal equivalence, 458 disqualified by the startup floor, 0 with no checked invariant, 935 publishable — of which 0 resting on a sole comparand and therefore uncorroborated, 82 inside the 20% noise band, 231 against a comparand that is not authoritative, and 623 that a conclusion may rest on.

## Engine-internal speedups over Zeebra

**A different statistic from the table above, and not a correction to it.** Each operand here is the library's **own reported stage time** for the task, so process startup, parsing and output are excluded from both sides. It is therefore **not an end-to-end cost** and may not be quoted as one: what a caller pays is the wall-clock table.

It exists because the startup-floor guard above correctly disqualifies every classical row this board holds — Singular's wall on everything up to `katsura-6` is its own `exec` — while the data for an engine comparison is already recorded. The floor guard is **not** relaxed; there is no startup in either operand of this ratio, so the condition that replaces it is one of **resolution**: a stage printed in whole milliseconds says nothing about a figure of one or two, and a reported figure must be at least 5x its own library's granularity. Each published figure carries the quantization uncertainty the coarser operand puts on it.

Stage selection per library, and what is excluded from each, is `engine_internal_time`; the granularities are `stage_resolution`. A library that reports no usable stage contributes no row, which is visible as an em dash in the measurement table's `stages` column.

**1346 pairs**: 0 refused for want of a recorded ideal equivalence, 26 refused on stage resolution, 0 with no checked invariant, 1320 publishable as an engine-internal comparison.
